NOTES ON GAMES AN.) stiTEILETICS.

RUGBY FOOTBALL.

The only attraction at Wilderspool on Saturday, was a match between local teams, Latchford Ranger( paying the "A " team a visit. This fixture was a rather inter- esting one as the Rangers have leaped into fame during the past month by carrying off the championship of Lan- cashire Junior Football, It is some satisfaction to know that there is yet a Warrington team capable of winning some sort of championship. Notwithstanding this the gate was smaller than was expected, and the majority of those present seemed to be Latchford partizans. The regular crowd appeared to prefer their coppers to seeing anything in football below first-class form.

Latchford Rangers are a burlier lot than I imagined and possess a fine set of forwards. They easly showed superiority in the scrimmages, and pushed the " A " team about in a fashion the latter are not accustomed to. When it seemed certain that a score would not be long delayed, a most unfortunate mishap betel Price,one of the strongest members of the Rangers' front rank. By some means or other he got his leg broken, and had to be taken from the field in a cab. Apart from a feeling of sorrow fer Price, the crowd were disapp Anted because of the inequality of the teams caused by the accident. Instead of seeing a trial of strength between the two teams, the Rangers' fourteen bad to nsake the beet show tney could against the" A "team's fifteen.

Though thus handicapped the Latchford:lads made a very good fight of it. and their opponents were fortunate in winning by a try to a nil. Play on both sides was far below what was expected, indeed neither team gave a true indication of their abilities. Passing was very poor and altogether the game was a disappointing one. It was wall in the second half before Duckworth scored for the "A" team and gave his side the victory. Jones was about the best man in the home team, and Raynor the worst. The others were very moderate. Ashurst, Rot- croft, and Houghton rendered the mast prominent ser- vices for the Rangers.

The work's competitioa has been a great success so far,and the crowds which have locked down to the Wil- derspool ground have been far in excess of previous years. some very good fights have been witnessed, and the play of some of the works' teams has been of a smart description. We have now got to the semi-finals, the first of which was played last evening between Pear- son and Knowles' aud Cheshire lines.

Oldham have won the championship of the Lancashire Senior Competition, and in no half-hearted fashion. At one time Swinton threatened to run their great rivals desperately des., but the brilliant victory Oldham gained over the" dark blues in the return engagement quite altered the aspect of affairs, and since then the progress of the leaders has been one continued triumph. Saturday was an eventful day to beth clubs, though few had any doubt as to the ultimate result. Thus if Swin- ton won at Widnes and Oldham suffered defeat by Tyl- desley the points would have been level. As might have been expected, however, quite the contrary happened. Oldham gaining& splendid victory, whilst Swinton suffered defeat by 5 points to 3. Looked at from every point of view, ttle record of the champions is a fine one. To have scored 47 points oat of a possible 52 is an achievement of which any club may be proud, and the disappointments suffered last season and the season before have now been amply atoned for.

CYCLING. Cyclists, particularly ladies, get cnore consideration nowadays frorr other users of the road than formerly ; but even now it is not easy to ride along a busy thoroug- fare for drivers of vehicles seem to differ in opinion as to their position on the road. Some will generously leave sufficient room on the left side for the cyclist, although it is the wrong side to pass a vehicle, while others prefer to keep class on their own side. The centre of the road is undoubtedly the best for heavy vehicles, and there they are generally to be found, des- pite any rule of the road.

R. L. Jefferson started for tus long ride to Kiiiva on Satutday.

It will be good news to all who journey into Derby- shire or Macclesfield way awheel to hear that the ter- rible paving formerly existing between Stockport and liazelgrove has in many parts been replaced by good setts, so that it is now possible to travel that way with !some degree of comfort

" Jep "Goecoyne and Barry grown, the speedy tan- dem pair of "pros," and their trainer, "Captain " Coe, are now staying in Wigan for the purpose of training on the cement track there.

The Lancashire and Cheshire District Association of tha C.T.C. will join the Liverpool Pieces C.C.. at tea, at Warrington, to-day.

The record season soon promisee to start in earnest. Dick Palmer shortly goes at the "five," whilst Betts will make a cut at ell the short distance records up to the hour when he is fit. Tandem figures, too, are likely to be cut, and thus early it Is safe to say that there is likely to be a general reduction in all the principal record figures.

A very enjoyable run was spent by the members of the Warriogton C.C.on Saturday.lt was a splendid day, and the roads were in good condition. Under these cir- curnotances the run was all the more appreciated, as the three or four previous runs had turned eat to be wet daye. About forty members took part in the run to linutsford and Altrinobatn. Tea was provided, at the ged Lion, Koutsford. The return journey was made by way of Tabby and Northwich.

On Thursday afternoon a building in course of erection in the neighbourhood of Victeria-it., London, collapsed. rive workmen are known te be killed, and it is feared 1%1141 ethers are haled in the building.
